\section*{v4.9.0-r37 Source-Only: Triality as Self-Traversal, and the Periods of the Structure}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{v4.9.0-r37 Source-Only: Triality as Self-Traversal, and the Periods of the Structure}
\label{sec:v490-r37-triality-self-traversal}
% ============================================================================

\paragraph{Purpose.}
Layer v4.9.0-r36 showed that inside \(\OO\) with \(e_7\) fixed every \(\mathbb Z_3\) symmetry is a colour transformation, so the
generations need a second three-fold structure.  The author states (2026-10-23) that the triole was meant as another
representation of the Sphaera, and that the fractal self-traversal transforms between such representations; the number of steps
is not to be fixed at three.  This layer records the pre-registered test H22 (note
\texttt{llm/Vorab\_Notiz\_H22\_Triality\_Selbstdurchwanderung.md}, commit \texttt{ed74608}, fixed before computing) on the triality
form of v4.8.0-r14 and on the periods available to the structure.  Earlier layers are not rewritten.

\noindent\textbf{Source-only status.}
This v4.9.0-r37 layer is source-only.  It does not build a PDF, does not assign a new DOI,
and does not authorize a public release.  The v4.9.0 Version DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23534954}{10.5281/zenodo.23534954} and the Concept DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19210728}{10.5281/zenodo.19210728} are unchanged by it.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r37 results}
\label{subsec:v490-r37-results}

Let \(t(x,y,z)=\operatorname{Re}((xy)z)\) on \(\OO^3\),
\(\mathfrak g=\{(A,B,C)\in\mathfrak{so}(8)^3: t(Ax,y,z)+t(x,By,z)+t(x,y,Cz)=0\}\), \(\tau(x,y,z)=(y,z,x)\) and
\(\rho(x,y,z)=(\bar x,\bar z,\bar y)\); \(T\), \(R_{\pi/2}\) act on the \(\mathbb C\) factor as in v4.9.0-r30.

\paragraph{Theoremlet v4.9.0-r37.1.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(a)] \(\dim\mathfrak g=28\); each projection \(\mathfrak g\to\mathfrak{so}(8)\) is an isomorphism, and the three representations
of \(\mathfrak g\) on the three slots are irreducible and pairwise inequivalent.  Thus \(t\) is the \(\mathrm{Spin}(8)\) triality form.
\item[(b)] \(\tau\) maps \(\mathfrak g\) to itself and permutes the three slot representations cyclically; it is an outer
automorphism of order three.
\item[(c)] The \(\tau\)-fixed elements \((A,A,A)\in\mathfrak g\) form the derivation algebra of \(\OO\), of dimension \(14\); those
with \(Ae_7=0\) form \(\mathfrak{su}(3)\).
\item[(d)] On \(\OO^4\) the five bracketings of \(\operatorname{Re}(xyzw)\) fall into two classes,
\(\operatorname{Re}(((xy)z)w)=\operatorname{Re}((xy)(zw))=\operatorname{Re}(x(y(zw)))\) and
\(\operatorname{Re}((x(yz))w)=\operatorname{Re}(x((yz)w))\).  The cyclic shift of the four slots exchanges the classes, so no single
bracketing is invariant, while their sum is.
\item[(e)] \(\tau,\rho,T,R_{\pi/2}\) generate on \(\mathbb C\otimes\OO^3\) a group of order \(48\), isomorphic to \(S_3\times D_4\), with
element orders \(1,2,3,4,6,12\); \(\tau\) and \(\rho\) preserve \(t\).
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Proof sketch.}
(a)--(c) by solving the linear conditions on \(\mathfrak{so}(8)^3\) (\(512\) equations, \(84\) unknowns) and computing ranks,
intertwiner spaces and commutants (script \texttt{llm/k78\_test\_h22.py}, deviations at most \(10^{-14}\)); \(\tau\) preserves \(t\)
because \(\operatorname{Re}((xy)z)=\operatorname{Re}((yz)x)\) (v4.8.0-r14.2), and an automorphism permuting inequivalent
representations is not inner.  (d) from \(\operatorname{Re}((ab)c)=\operatorname{Re}(a(bc))\) and \(\operatorname{Re}(ab)=\operatorname{Re}(ba)\),
checked numerically; the exchange of the classes was found in a check after the evaluation.  (e) by enumerating the group.
\(\square\)

\paragraph{Verdict and reading.}
H22a, b, c and e are confirmed.  H22d, the statement that no single bracketing is cyclically invariant, is confirmed, but by (d)
it gives no prohibition of a fourth slot.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item The second three-fold structure required by v4.9.0-r36 exists: it is triality.  The three slots of \(\OO^3\) carry the three
inequivalent eight-dimensional representations of \(\mathrm{Spin}(8)\), the shift of the slots is the outer triality automorphism,
and what it fixes is \(G_2\supset SU(3)_c\).  In this reading generation is what the traversal permutes and colour is what it
fixes; they are orbit and fixed group of one motion, no longer one object.  It matches the author's reading of the triole as
three representations of the same \(\OO\cong\mathbb R^8\).  That the slots \emph{are} the physical generations is not shown.
\item That there are exactly three rests on the known classification: \(\mathrm{Spin}(8)\) has exactly three eight-dimensional
irreducible representations and \(\operatorname{Out}(\mathrm{Spin}(8))=S_3\).  (a) is consistent with it.  It excludes a fourth slot
with a new eight-dimensional representation, not a repetition of one of the three.  The structure therefore does not by itself
forbid a fourth generation.
\item The structure has no distinguished period.  Period two belongs to \(\rho\), \(T\), \(J_B\) and the alternating triolic shift of
\S\ref{sec:alternating-triolic-orbit-scan}, period three to triality, period four to the duality rotation \(R_{\pi/2}\), and six and
twelve to their combinations.  In the author's picture, the period seen by a readout is a choice of subgroup.
\item The word triole names at least three structures in the corpus: the frame triole matter--photon--tachyon of the original
paper (spring theorem; \(\mathbb Z_2\) with fixed point, amplitudes \(1:1:3\)), the kernel triole with \(120^\circ\) phases (a colour
rotation inside \(\OO\), v4.9.0-r36), and the slot triole of \(\OO^3\) (triality).  The double uses found in v4.8.0-r13 and
v4.9.0-r34 to r36 arise where these are identified.
\end{enumerate}

\section*{v4.9.0-r38 Source-Only: The Frame Triole as the Reflection Side of Triality}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{v4.9.0-r38 Source-Only: The Frame Triole as the Reflection Side of Triality}
\label{sec:v490-r38-frame-triole-triality}
% ============================================================================

\paragraph{Purpose.}
In the original paper the triole of the spring theorem is the triple matter--photon--tachyon: the photon is the equilibrium node
and matter and tachyon are mirror images.  Layer v4.9.0-r37 found that the cyclic shift \(\tau\) of the three slots of \(\OO^3\) is
triality.  This layer records the pre-registered test H23 (note \texttt{llm/Vorab\_Notiz\_H23\_Frame\_Triole\_Triality.md}, commit
\texttt{55043f8}, fixed before computing): whether the frame triole has the form of the second slot map
\(\rho(x,y,z)=(\bar x,\bar z,\bar y)\), which fixes one slot and exchanges the other two.  Earlier layers are not rewritten.

\noindent\textbf{Source-only status.}
This v4.9.0-r38 layer is source-only.  It does not build a PDF, does not assign a new DOI,
and does not authorize a public release.  The v4.9.0 Version DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23534954}{10.5281/zenodo.23534954} and the Concept DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19210728}{10.5281/zenodo.19210728} are unchanged by it.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r38 results}
\label{subsec:v490-r38-results}

Notation as in v4.9.0-r37; \(c\) is octonionic conjugation, and \(\rho\) acts on \(\mathfrak g\) by
\((A,B,C)\mapsto(cAc,cCc,cBc)\).

\paragraph{Theoremlet v4.9.0-r38.1.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(a)] \(\rho\) is an automorphism of \(\mathfrak g\) of order two.  It keeps the representation class of slot~1 and exchanges the
classes of slots~2 and~3; it is outer.
\item[(b)] The \(\rho\)-fixed subalgebra of \(\mathfrak g\) is \(21\)-dimensional, isomorphic to \(\mathfrak{so}(7)\).  On slot~1 it acts as
\(1\oplus7\) with invariant line \(\mathbb R\cdot1\) and irreducible complement; on slots~2 and~3 it acts irreducibly, by equivalent
eight-dimensional representations.
\item[(c)] The sign triples preserving \(t\) are those with \(s_1s_2s_3=1\), a group \(\mathbb Z_2\times\mathbb Z_2\); the one trivial on
slot~1 is \((1,-1,-1)\).
\item[(d)] \(\tau\) and \(\rho\) generate \(S_3\) on the slots; its \(\mathbb Z_3\) has one orbit of length three, each transposition a
fixed slot and an orbit of length two.
\item[(e)] \(\mathfrak g_2=\{(A,A,A)\}\) is \(\rho\)-fixed and lies in the \(\mathfrak{so}(7)\) of (b).
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Proof sketch.}
(a), (b), (e) by linear algebra on \(\mathfrak{so}(8)^3\): closure under \(\rho\), intertwiner spaces between the slot representations
of \(\mathfrak g\) and of its image, the fixed space of \(\rho\), and commutants (script \texttt{llm/k79\_test\_h23.py}, deviations at most
\(10^{-15}\)).  (c) is immediate from trilinearity and was checked numerically; (d) by enumeration.  \(\square\)

\paragraph{Verdict and reading.}
H23a--e are confirmed; (c) and (d) are elementary.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item The \(120^\circ\) triole is the \(\mathbb Z_3\)-orbit of triality; the frame triole of the original paper has the form of a
transposition, one fixed slot and two exchanged.  Both are views of the same \(S_3\) on the three triality slots.  In the author's
picture, the zoom chooses the subgroup.
\item Reading: photon = the fixed slot, which under the fixed \(\mathrm{Spin}(7)\) has a distinguished direction, the real unit;
matter and tachyon = the two exchanged slots, equal under \(\mathrm{Spin}(7)\) and mirrored by \(\rho\).  The parity \((1,-1,-1)\)
separates the photon slot from the other two in the way \((-1)^F\) separates bosons from fermions; it is trivial, and notable only
because it fits this reading.
\item Which slot is the photon is a choice: \(S_3\) has three transpositions, each fixing a different slot.  This agrees with
Convention~F, where the photon is the degenerate fixed point.
\item Colour is fixed by the whole \(S_3\) (e).
\item Open: on one level the three slots cannot carry both the frames (this layer) and the generations (reading of v4.9.0-r37).  In
the author's picture these would be two zoom levels.  Which level carries which role is not shown; \(\operatorname{Im}\HH\) of the
Dixon storage (v4.9.0-r33) is a second three-fold carrier.
\item In eight dimensions the two spinor slots are a chirality pair.  If matter and tachyon are read as these, the frame change
\(M\leftrightarrow T\) is a change of chirality.  This is a lead for the chirality test of the inventory, not computed here.
\end{enumerate}

\section*{v4.9.0-r39 Source-Only: Chains of Different Length under Hilbert-Shift Zoom and Mixed Readouts}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{v4.9.0-r39 Source-Only: Chains of Different Length under Hilbert-Shift Zoom and Mixed Readouts}
\label{sec:v490-r39-chains-zoom-mixed}
% ============================================================================

\paragraph{Purpose.}
The author states (2026-10-23) that the mixed readouts were meant for the case in which the generations form a circle after
repeated zoom, and that chains of different length might give different readings.  This layer records the pre-registered test H24
(note \texttt{llm/Vorab\_Notiz\_H24\_Ketten\_Module\_Readouts.md}, commit \texttt{8c89481}, fixed before computing).  It uses the
Hilbert shift of Definition~\ref{def:qam-hilbert-shift-plan} and the bit registry \(\beta_j=2^j\) of
Definition~\ref{def:binary-mask-registry-mixed-readouts}.  Earlier layers are not rewritten.

\noindent\textbf{Source-only status.}
This v4.9.0-r39 layer is source-only.  It does not build a PDF, does not assign a new DOI,
and does not authorize a public release.  The v4.9.0 Version DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23534954}{10.5281/zenodo.23534954} and the Concept DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19210728}{10.5281/zenodo.19210728} are unchanged by it.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r39 model}
\label{subsec:v490-r39-model}

A chain of length \(m\) is a cyclic group \(\langle g\rangle\) with \(\operatorname{ord}g=m\).  A readout of level \(j\) (bit \(2^j\)) sees
\(\langle g^{2^j}\rangle\).  A mixed mask \(S\) with \(k=\sum_{j\in S}2^j\) is read in two ways: (\(\Sigma\)) as one step,
\(\langle g^k\rangle\), or (\(\vee\)) as the joint readout \(\langle g^{2^j}:j\in S\rangle\).  The group is
\(G=\langle\tau,\rho,T,R_{\pi/2}\rangle\cong S_3\times D_4\) of v4.9.0-r37.  The identification of zoom with the step \(2^j\) is a reading.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r39 results}
\label{subsec:v490-r39-results}

\paragraph{Theoremlet v4.9.0-r39.1.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(a)] A chain of odd length keeps its length at every level.  For the triality, \(\tau^{2^j}=\tau\) for even \(j\) and
\(\tau^{2^j}=\tau^{-1}=\rho\tau\rho\) for odd \(j\).
\item[(b)] A chain of length \(2^a\) has length \(2^{a-j}\) at level \(j<a\) and \(1\) from level \(a\) on.  In \(G\) the visible orders are
\(\{1,2,3,4,6,12\}\) at level \(0\), \(\{1,2,3,6\}\) at level \(1\), and \(\{1,3\}\) from level \(2\) on, where every \(g^{2^j}\) lies in
\(\langle\tau\rangle\).
\item[(c)] For \(m=3\) and masks over six bits: in reading (\(\Sigma\)) the \(21\) masks with \(3\mid k\), for instance \(S=\{0,1\}\), see one
generation and the other \(42\) see three; in reading (\(\vee\)) every mask sees three.
\item[(d)] For every \(m\): (\(\Sigma\)) the visible length is \(m/\gcd(m,k)\); (\(\vee\)) it is \(m/\gcd(m,2^{\min S})\), so the odd part of \(m\)
is always preserved.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Proof sketch.}
Elementary: \(2^j\) is invertible modulo odd \(m\), and \(2^j\equiv(-1)^j\pmod 3\); the subgroup of \(\mathbb Z_m\) generated by a set of
steps has order \(m/\gcd\).  All statements were checked by exact enumeration, on \(G\) as matrices, for \(m\le64\) and all \(63\) masks
(script \texttt{llm/k80\_test\_h24.py}).  \(\square\)

\paragraph{Verdict and reading.}
H24a--e are confirmed; the content is the arithmetic of cyclic groups, and the physics lies only in the reading.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item In the model, the generations do form a circle under repeated zoom, and it is the only thing left: from level \(2\) on, only the
triality circle of length three remains visible, while the frame exchange (period \(2\)) and the duality rotation (period \(4\))
disappear.  This separates the two levels left open in v4.9.0-r38: the frames at coarse zoom, the generations at deep zoom.
\item On odd levels the circle runs backwards, and the reversed circle is the frame mirror \(\rho\); every second level reads the
generations in the mirrored frame.
\item What survives zoom is the odd part of a chain length.  Chains of length \(6\) (three generations times two frames) or \(12\) show
three at deep zoom; chains of length \(2^a\) vanish.  No generation chain of length four or more arises in \(G\).
\item Mixed readouts give different readings depending on the aggregation: as one step, a third of the masks see one generation; as
a joint readout, all see three.  Definition~\ref{def:guarded-mixed-qam-readout} leaves the aggregation \(\mu\) open.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Author remark and textual reading.}
The author states (2026-10-23) that the contraction discipline of Definition~\ref{def:two-sided-admissible-contractions} was
introduced so that classical tensor calculus keeps working while mixed channels can also be contracted, that it was hardly used, and
that the paper was written from one frame.  Read against r39.1: in the selector contraction \(\chi_{mn}R^mS^n\) of
Definition~\ref{def:guarded-two-sided-mixed-qam-readout}, compatible modes are composed; for shift readouts
\(R^m=g^{2^m}\), \(S^n=g^{2^n}\) the composition is \(g^{2^m+2^n}\), which is reading (\(\Sigma\)).  The Boolean mask grammar
(\(\chi_A\vee\chi_B\)) only selects the active kinds, which is reading (\(\vee\)).  Both readings are therefore present in the text, at
different places.  Combining an even and an odd level by contraction gives \(\tau^{3}=1\): a readout composed with its frame mirror
does not see the generation circle.  The front indices of Definition~\ref{def:two-sided-structural-tensor-interface} carry the frame;
in the main body they are not varied, which is the one-frame restriction.  This is a reading, not part of the pre-registered test.

\section*{v4.9.0-r40 Source-Only: The Two-Sided Contraction across Frames, and the Weak SU(2)}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{v4.9.0-r40 Source-Only: The Two-Sided Contraction across Frames, and the Weak SU(2)}
\label{sec:v490-r40-two-sided-frames}
% ============================================================================

\paragraph{Purpose.}
The two-sided notation of Definition~\ref{def:two-sided-structural-tensor-interface} puts the frame on the front indices.  The author
states (2026-10-23) that its contraction discipline, Definition~\ref{def:two-sided-admissible-contractions}, has never been used or
tested, and that it is not expected to work at once.  This layer uses the front side across frames for the first time and records
the pre-registered test H25 (note \texttt{llm/Vorab\_Notiz\_H25\_Zweiseitige\_Kontraktion\_Frames.md}, commit \texttt{d1aba48}, fixed
before computing).  It also asks whether a weak \(SU(2)\) commuting with colour exists in the triality algebra.  Earlier layers are not
rewritten.

\noindent\textbf{Source-only status.}
This v4.9.0-r40 layer is source-only.  It does not build a PDF, does not assign a new DOI,
and does not authorize a public release.  The v4.9.0 Version DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23534954}{10.5281/zenodo.23534954} and the Concept DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19210728}{10.5281/zenodo.19210728} are unchanged by it.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r40 model}
\label{subsec:v490-r40-model}

Front side: the \(\mathbb C\) factor of the Dixon storage, with the frame maps \(R_{\pi/2}\) (linear, \(X\mapsto iX\), contragredient
\(\omega\mapsto-i\omega\)), \(T\) (antilinear, complex conjugation) and \(J_B=R_{\pi/2}\circ T\).  Back side: real structure, acting on
the index \(\alpha\).  Rule~1 is the \(\mathbb C\)-bilinear contraction \(\omega_\alpha X^\alpha\); rule~2 is \(q(X)=G_{\alpha\beta}X^\alpha X^\beta\)
with real symmetric \(G\), compared with the Hermitian form \(h(X)=G_{\alpha\beta}\bar X^\alpha X^\beta\).  On
\(W=\mathbb C\otimes\HH\otimes\OO\) the front maps are \(D_4\) and left multiplication \(L_q\) by unit quaternions, the back maps right
multiplication \(R_q\) and \(SU(3)_c=\operatorname{Stab}_{G_2}(e_7)\).

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r40 results}
\label{subsec:v490-r40-results}

\paragraph{Theoremlet v4.9.0-r40.1.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(a)] On \(W\) every front map commutes with every back map.
\item[(b)] \(\omega_\alpha X^\alpha\) is invariant under \(R_{\pi/2}\) and is complex conjugated under \(T\) and \(J_B\).
\item[(c)] \(q\mapsto-q\) under \(R_{\pi/2}\), \(q\mapsto\bar q\) under \(T\), \(q\mapsto-\bar q\) under \(J_B\); \(h\) is invariant under all eight
elements of \(D_4\).
\item[(d)] The centralizer of \(\mathfrak{su}(3)_c\) in the triality algebra \(\mathfrak g\cong\mathfrak{so}(8)\) of v4.9.0-r37 is two-dimensional
and abelian.  On \(W\), \(L_q\) and \(R_q\) give two copies of \(\mathfrak{su}(2)\) that commute with each other, with
\(\mathfrak{su}(3)_c\) and with \(D_4\).
\item[(e)] No nonzero element of \(\mathfrak g\) acts trivially on one spinor slot and nontrivially on the other.
\end{enumerate}
In a check after the evaluation, the centralizer in (d) was found to be \(\operatorname{span}(L_{e_7},R_{e_7})\) on each slot.

\paragraph{Proof sketch.}
(b), (c) by direct substitution, checked on random vectors; \(D_4\) is generated by \(R_{\pi/2}\) and \(T\).  (a), (d), (e) by matrices
and null spaces (script \texttt{llm/k81\_test\_h25.py}, deviations at most \(10^{-14}\)); (e) is the injectivity of the slot projections
(v4.9.0-r37.1(a)).  \(\square\)

\paragraph{Verdict and reading.}
H25a--e are confirmed; (a) and (e) are controls.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item Used across frames for the first time, the rules work, but not as stated.  Rule~1 says that the classical contraction remains
unchanged; across frames this holds for \(R_{\pi/2}\) only, while the antilinear frame maps conjugate the value.  Only real readouts are
frame-invariant.  Rule~2 depends on whether the comparison is bilinear or Hermitian, which the definition does not fix.  The notation
is frame-covariant, not frame-invariant; this is a finding about the notation, which so far was used in one frame only.
\item The bilinear comparison changes sign under the transition \(z\mapsto iz\) from M to T.  Read as a squared mass, this is
\(m^2\mapsto-m^2\), the tachyon.  The Hermitian comparison does not distinguish M from T.  The choice is therefore one of content:
bilinear comparisons separate the frames, Hermitian ones do not.
\item No \(\mathfrak{su}(2)\) of the triality algebra commutes with colour; what commutes is \(\operatorname{span}(L_{e_7},R_{e_7})\), the
generators of the charge \(Q=\tfrac12-\tfrac{i}{6}L_{e_7}-\tfrac{i}{3}R_{e_7}\) of v4.9.0-r35.  The triality level carries colour and
charge, but no weak interaction.  An \(\mathfrak{su}(2)\) commuting with colour appears only in the \(\HH\) factor, twice: from the left
(front side) and from the right (back side).
\item Reading: the two \(\mathfrak{su}(2)\) of \(\HH\) sit on the two sides of the two-sided notation, and ``the weak \(SU(2)\) acts only
on the left'' would read ``the weak \(SU(2)\) is one side''.  This is not shown.  The conflict of the inventory remains: the left side
also carries the Lorentz rotation, and the right side the generation \(\mathbb Z_3\) of v4.9.0-r33.
\item Proposed frame clause for rules~1 and~2 (author decision): readouts are real or are conjugated with the antilinear frame maps,
and each comparison tensor is declared bilinear (frame-separating) or Hermitian (frame-blind).
\end{enumerate}

\section*{v4.9.0-r41 Source-Only: Distributing the Roles of the Quaternion Factor; Hypercharge and Electric Charge}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{v4.9.0-r41 Source-Only: Distributing the Roles of the Quaternion Factor; Hypercharge and Electric Charge}
\label{sec:v490-r41-quaternion-roles}
% ============================================================================

\paragraph{Purpose.}
The inventory of the standard-model features found the quaternion factor overloaded: it carries the Lorentz spinor
(Theorem~\ref{thm:three-generations}), the weak \(SU(2)\) (Theorem~\ref{thm:SM}) and, in v4.9.0-r33, the generation index.  Layers
v4.9.0-r37 to r40 found a carrier for the generations in triality, a separation of frames and generations by zoom level, and two
\(\mathfrak{su}(2)\) commuting with colour, from the left and from the right of \(\HH\).  This layer records the pre-registered test H26
(note \texttt{llm/Vorab\_Notiz\_H26\_Verteilung\_H\_Faktor.md}, commit \texttt{1876b55}, fixed before computing) of a distribution D of
these roles.  The charge pattern of (b) and (c) below was seen analytically before the note and is disclosed there; the computation
checks it and is not a prediction.  Earlier layers are not rewritten.

\noindent\textbf{Source-only status.}
This v4.9.0-r41 layer is source-only.  It does not build a PDF, does not assign a new DOI,
and does not authorize a public release.  The v4.9.0 Version DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23534954}{10.5281/zenodo.23534954} and the Concept DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19210728}{10.5281/zenodo.19210728} are unchanged by it.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r41 distribution D}
\label{subsec:v490-r41-distribution}

Lorentz \(SL(2,\mathbb C)\) by left multiplication \(L_q\) (rotations) and \(iL_q\) (boosts) on \(\mathbb C\otimes\HH\); weak \(SU(2)\) by
right multiplication \(R_q\) on \(\HH\), with \(T_3=-\tfrac{i}{2}R_k\); colour \(SU(3)_c\) on \(\OO\); frames \(D_4\) on \(\mathbb C\); generations on
the triality slots of \(\OO^3\).  Hypercharge \(Y/2:=Q-\tfrac12\) with \(Q\) of v4.9.0-r34/r35; electric charge \(Q_{\rm em}:=T_3+Y/2\).  Here \(i\)
is the outer unit.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r41 results}
\label{subsec:v490-r41-results}

\paragraph{Theoremlet v4.9.0-r41.1.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(a)] On \(\mathbb C\otimes\HH\otimes\OO\) the weak \(SU(2)\) commutes with the Lorentz group, with colour and with \(Q\).  The Lorentz
rotations are fixed by \(T\); the boosts are reversed by \(T\).
\item[(b)] The joint eigenspaces of \(Q\) and \(T_3\) carry \(Q_{\rm em}\) as follows:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}llll@{}}
\toprule
\(Q\)-sector (colour) & \(Y/2\) & \(Q_{\rm em}\) at \(T_3=+\tfrac12,-\tfrac12\) & reading\\
\midrule
\(0\) (singlet) & \(-\tfrac12\) & \(0,\ -1\) & lepton doublet \((\nu,e)\)\\
\(\tfrac23\) (triplet) & \(+\tfrac16\) & \(\tfrac23,\ -\tfrac13\) & quark doublet \((u,d)\)\\
\(\tfrac13\) (conjugate triplet) & \(-\tfrac16\) & \(\tfrac13,\ -\tfrac23\) & antiquark doublet \((\bar d,\bar u)\)\\
\(1\) (singlet) & \(+\tfrac12\) & \(1,\ 0\) & antilepton doublet \((e^+,\bar\nu)\)\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\item[(c)] \(TQ_{\rm em}T^{-1}=-Q_{\rm em}\) and \(T(Y/2)T^{-1}=-Y/2\).
\item[(d)] There is no weak singlet: \(T_1^2+T_2^2+T_3^2=\tfrac34\) on the whole space.
\item[(e)] On \(\mathbb C\otimes\HH\otimes\OO^3\) the cyclic shift of the slots commutes with \(L_q\), \(R_q\), colour and \(Q\).
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Proof sketch.}
By explicit complex matrices on \(\HH\otimes\OO\cong\mathbb C^{32}\), with the \(\mathbb C\) factor as scalar and \(T\) as complex conjugation
(script \texttt{llm/k82\_test\_h26.py}, deviations at most \(10^{-14}\)).  (c) follows from \(TQT^{-1}=1-Q\) (v4.9.0-r34.1(d)) and
\(T(-\tfrac i2R_k)T^{-1}=\tfrac i2R_k\).  (d): \(R_q\) acts on \(\HH\cong\mathbb C^2\otimes\mathbb C^2\) as a doublet on the right index.  \(\square\)

\paragraph{Verdict and reading.}
H26a--e are confirmed; (d) is negative for D.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item In D the quaternion factor is no longer overloaded: the Lorentz group sits on the left (front side), the weak \(SU(2)\) on the
right (back side), the generations on the triality slots.  The zoom levels separate frames from generations; the sides separate the
Lorentz group from the weak \(SU(2)\).
\item Under the choice \(Y/2=Q-\tfrac12\), \(Q_{\rm em}=T_3+Y/2\) gives exactly the hypercharges and charges of the left-handed
standard-model doublets and their conjugates, with the right colour: leptons singlets, quarks triplets, antiquarks conjugate
triplets.  The choice is motivated by the pattern and not forced by the structure.  The sector assignment is Furey's; similar patterns
are known in the literature on \(\mathbb C\otimes\HH\otimes\OO\).  New here is the placement on the sides and frames of the Companion.
\item In D the frame change \(T\) is charge conjugation, \(Q_{\rm em}\mapsto-Q_{\rm em}\).  The shift in \(Q\mapsto1-Q\) of v4.9.0-r34.1(d) is
the hypercharge offset \(\tfrac12\).  Matter frame and tachyon frame behave in D as particle and antiparticle.
\item D is incomplete.  Every state is a weak doublet; the right-handed singlets \(u_R,d_R,e_R,\nu_R\), half of a generation, are absent,
and all states carry the same left Lorentz index.  Chirality is not explained.  Weak singlets would need a structure on which the right
action of \(\HH\) is trivial on part of the space, for instance a projector coupling left and right.
\end{enumerate}

\section*{v4.9.0-r42 Source-Only: A Compensator of the Millennium Matrix as Chirality Projector}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{v4.9.0-r42 Source-Only: A Compensator of the Millennium Matrix as Chirality Projector}
\label{sec:v490-r42-mm-compensator-chirality}
% ============================================================================

\paragraph{Purpose.}
Layer v4.9.0-r41 placed the weak \(SU(2)\) on the right of \(\HH\) and obtained only weak doublets.  The author states (2026-10-23) that
the Millennium matrix of \S\ref{sec:millennium-hc}, which was to carry several Heisenberg compensators, was meant for the missing half.
This layer records the pre-registered test H27 (note \texttt{llm/Vorab\_Notiz\_H27\_MM\_Kompensator\_Chiralitaet.md}, commit
\texttt{614ac3e}, fixed before computing).  The charge pattern was seen analytically before the note and is disclosed there; the
computation checks it and is not a prediction.  Earlier layers are not rewritten.

\noindent\textbf{Source-only status.}
This v4.9.0-r42 layer is source-only.  It does not build a PDF, does not assign a new DOI,
and does not authorize a public release.  The v4.9.0 Version DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23534954}{10.5281/zenodo.23534954} and the Concept DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19210728}{10.5281/zenodo.19210728} are unchanged by it.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r42 construction}
\label{subsec:v490-r42-construction}

On \(\mathbb C\otimes\HH\otimes\OO\cong\mathbb C^{32}\) with the distribution D of v4.9.0-r41, put
\[
C_{\rm ch}=\tfrac12(\mathrm{id}+J),\qquad J=i\,L_{e_7},\qquad T_a'=C_{\rm ch}T_a,
\]
\[
\tfrac{Y}{2}=Q-\tfrac12+(1-C_{\rm ch})T_3,\qquad Q_{\rm em}=T_3+Q-\tfrac12 ,
\]
with \(i\) the outer unit and \(L_{e_7}\) the inner unit, so \(J^2=1\) and \(C_{\rm ch}\) has the form of the Heisenberg compensator.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r42 results}
\label{subsec:v490-r42-results}

\paragraph{Theoremlet v4.9.0-r42.1.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(a)] \(C_{\rm ch}\) is a Hermitian projector of rank \(16\); it commutes with colour, the Lorentz group, the right action of \(\HH\)
and \(Q\), and its image is the sum of the \(Q\)-sectors \(0\) and \(\tfrac23\).
\item[(b)] Under colour, the weak \(SU(2)'\) and \(Y/2\) the space splits into
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}llll@{}}
\toprule
\((SU(3),SU(2)',Y/2)\) & \(Q_{\rm em}\) & state & complex dimension\\
\midrule
\((3,2,\tfrac16)\) & \(\tfrac23,-\tfrac13\) & \((u,d)_L\) & \(12\)\\
\((1,2,-\tfrac12)\) & \(0,-1\) & \((\nu,e)_L\) & \(4\)\\
\((\bar3,1,-\tfrac23)\) & \(-\tfrac23\) & \(u^c\) & \(6\)\\
\((\bar3,1,\tfrac13)\) & \(\tfrac13\) & \(d^c\) & \(6\)\\
\((1,1,1)\) & \(1\) & \(e^c\) & \(2\)\\
\((1,1,0)\) & \(0\) & \(\nu^c\) & \(2\)\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
that is, sixteen Weyl states with two Lorentz components each; \(Y/2\) commutes with \(SU(2)'\), colour and the Lorentz group, and
\(Q_{\rm em}=T_3'+Y/2\).
\item[(c)] Over the sixteen states, \(\operatorname{Tr}Y\), \(\operatorname{Tr}Y^3\) and the \(SU(2)^2U(1)\) and \(SU(3)^2U(1)\) traces vanish.
\item[(d)] \(SU(2)'\) acts only on the image of \(C_{\rm ch}\), and \(TC_{\rm ch}T^{-1}=1-C_{\rm ch}\).
\item[(e)] \(\mathcal M=\lambda_0C_{\rm ch}+\lambda_1(1-C_{\rm ch})\), \(\lambda_0\neq\lambda_1\), has \(C_{\rm ch}\) as spectral projector, as in
Theorem~\ref{thm:hc-spectral-projector}.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Proof sketch.}
By explicit complex matrices (script \texttt{llm/k83\_test\_h27.py}, deviations at most \(10^{-15}\)).  \(C_{\rm ch}\) equals \(P_-\) of
v4.9.0-r35.1(c).  A first run reported (b) as refuted because the candidate values of \(Y/2\) were rounded to nine digits while the
null-space test required \(10^{-10}\), which lost all non-dyadic values; only this rounding was corrected, and both outputs are kept.
\(\square\)

\paragraph{Verdict and reading.}
H27a--e are confirmed, (b) after the correction of the script; (e) is a control.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item A compensator of the Companion's form \(\tfrac12(\mathrm{id}+J)\), with \(J\) the product of the outer and the inner unit, completes
the doublets of v4.9.0-r41 to the full left-handed content of one generation with right-handed neutrino.  The weak \(SU(2)\) acts only
on its image: in the model, this is chirality.  A compensator of the Millennium matrix separates the chiralities.
\item The frame change \(T\) exchanges image and kernel of the compensator.  With v4.9.0-r41.1(c), matter frame and tachyon frame are in
the model particle and antiparticle with mirrored chirality.  This is a reading.
\item The Millennium matrix of \S\ref{sec:millennium-hc} is \(16\times16\), and one generation with \(\nu_R\) has sixteen Weyl states.  This is
recorded, not interpreted.
\item This is a construction under choices, not a derivation: \(J\) and the formula for \(Y/2\) are chosen so that the known result
appears, and the pattern was seen in advance.  It shows that the building blocks of the Companion (compensator, two units, two sides,
triality) suffice.  Anomaly cancellation follows from the content.  Similar constructions exist in the literature on
\(\mathbb C\otimes\HH\otimes\OO\).  Why this choice, symmetry breaking, masses and mixings are not addressed.
\end{enumerate}

\label{subsec:v490-r42-frames}

result & M / T / P\\
\midrule

r42.1(d) & relates M and T: \(T\) exchanges the chiral halves; with r41.1(c) the T frame reads the conjugate states with mirrored chirality; P not evaluated\\

\section*{v4.9.0-r43 Source-Only: The Choice of the Chirality Projector Is Fixed by Anomaly Cancellation}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{v4.9.0-r43 Source-Only: The Choice of the Chirality Projector Is Fixed by Anomaly Cancellation}
\label{sec:v490-r43-choice-anomalies}
% ============================================================================

\paragraph{Purpose.}
Layer v4.9.0-r42 built the content of one generation with the compensator \(C=\tfrac12(\mathrm{id}+J)\), \(J=iL_{e_7}\), and the hypercharge
offset \(-\tfrac12\), both chosen.  The author asks why this choice.  This layer records the pre-registered test H28 (note
\texttt{llm/Vorab\_Notiz\_H28\_Warum\_diese\_Wahl.md}, commit \texttt{9b8b360}, fixed before computing; one case and the sector
assignment were computed by hand beforehand and are disclosed there).  Earlier layers are not rewritten.

\noindent\textbf{Source-only status.}
This v4.9.0-r43 layer is source-only.  It does not build a PDF, does not assign a new DOI,
and does not authorize a public release.  The v4.9.0 Version DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23534954}{10.5281/zenodo.23534954} and the Concept DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19210728}{10.5281/zenodo.19210728} are unchanged by it.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r43 setting}
\label{subsec:v490-r43-setting}

On \(\mathbb C\otimes\HH\otimes\OO\) as in v4.9.0-r42, a chirality projector is \(C=\tfrac12(1+J)\) with \(J\) Hermitian, \(J^2=1\), \(J\neq\pm1\),
acting on \(\mathbb C\otimes\OO\), commuting with colour and \(Q\), of rank \(16\), with image one colour singlet and one colour triplet.  The weak
\(SU(2)'\) is \(CT_a\), the charge \(Q_{\rm em}=T_3+Q+s\), the hypercharge \(Y/2=Q+s+(1-C)T_3\).

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r43 results}
\label{subsec:v490-r43-results}

\paragraph{Theoremlet v4.9.0-r43.1.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(a)] The commutant of colour and \(Q\) on \(\mathbb C\otimes\OO\) is spanned by the four sector projectors.  There are exactly four chirality
projectors, with \(J=iL_{e_7}\) (image the sectors \(0,\tfrac23\)), \(-iL_{e_7}\) (\(\tfrac13,1\)), \(iR_{e_7}\) (\(0,\tfrac13\)) and \(-iR_{e_7}\)
(\(\tfrac23,1\)).
\item[(b)] For each of them \(\operatorname{Tr}Y=0\) holds exactly for \(s=-\tfrac12\).
\item[(c)] At \(s=-\tfrac12\) all four anomaly traces (\(\operatorname{Tr}Y\), \(\operatorname{Tr}Y^3\), \(SU(2)^2U(1)\), \(SU(3)^2U(1)\)) vanish
exactly for \(J=\pm iL_{e_7}\).  For \(J=\pm iR_{e_7}\) one finds \(\operatorname{Tr}Y^3=\pm\tfrac32\) and \(SU(2)^2U(1)=\mp2\), the latter
summed over both states of each doublet.
\item[(d)] \(T(iL_{e_7})T^{-1}=-iL_{e_7}\): the two admissible choices are frame images of each other.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Proof sketch.}
By explicit matrices and exact fractions (script \texttt{llm/k84\_test\_h28.py}).  The commutant consists of scalars on the four \(Q\)-sectors,
because each sector is colour-irreducible with its own charge; a Hermitian involution there is a choice of sign per sector, and the rank and
image conditions leave four.  \(\square\)

\paragraph{Verdict and reading.}
H28a--d are confirmed.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item Under the assumptions of the setting the choice of v4.9.0-r42 is forced up to the frame.  Colour and charge conservation allow
four chirality projectors, the gravitational anomaly fixes the hypercharge offset \(-\tfrac12\), and the remaining anomalies select left
multiplication by \(e_7\).  What remains is the Standard Model and its frame mirror.  The selection is again one of left against right.
\item The setting itself stays an assumption: the weak \(SU(2)\) on the right of \(\HH\), \(Q_{\rm em}=T_3+Q+s\), and the projector acting on
\(\mathbb C\otimes\OO\).  Once these are made, nothing is left to choose.
\item This is no prediction; that the Standard Model is anomaly-free is known.  New is only that, in this structure, anomaly cancellation
alone decides between left and right.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r43 status in the three frames}
\label{subsec:v490-r43-frames}

\section*{v4.9.0-r44 Source-Only: Mass Terms as Frame Couplings; Breaking Stages for Masses and Mixing}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{v4.9.0-r44 Source-Only: Mass Terms as Frame Couplings; Breaking Stages for Masses and Mixing}
\label{sec:v490-r44-mass-frame-couplings}
% ============================================================================

\paragraph{Purpose.}
After v4.9.0-r42 and r43 the content of one generation stands, but symmetry breaking, masses and mixing are open.  This layer records the
pre-registered test H29 (note \texttt{llm/Vorab\_Notiz\_H29\_Brechung\_Massen\_Mischung.md}, commit \texttt{85d065a}, fixed before
computing).  It asks which maps between the two chiral halves are compatible with colour and the Lorentz group, which quantum numbers they
carry, and what the three triality slots imply for masses and mixing.  The known parts are disclosed in the note.  Earlier layers are not
rewritten.

\noindent\textbf{Source-only status.}
This v4.9.0-r44 layer is source-only.  It does not build a PDF, does not assign a new DOI,
and does not authorize a public release.  The v4.9.0 Version DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23534954}{10.5281/zenodo.23534954} and the Concept DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19210728}{10.5281/zenodo.19210728} are unchanged by it.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r44 setting}
\label{subsec:v490-r44-setting}

On \(V=\mathbb C\otimes\HH\otimes\OO\) as in v4.9.0-r42, a \emph{connector} is a map \(V\to V\) commuting with colour and with the Lorentz
rotations \(L_q\): linear (\(X\)) or antilinear (\(\Phi=X\circ T\)).  An antilinear connector anticommutes with the boosts \(iL_q\), so it maps
left- to right-handed spinors: it is the operator form of a mass term.  A Hermitian generator \(G\) acts by \(X\mapsto GX-XG\) (linear) or
\(X\mapsto GX+X\bar G\) (antilinear).  Blocks refer to \(C=C_{\rm ch}\).  On the triality slots a coupling is a Hermitian \(3\times3\) matrix
\(M\); \(P\) is the cyclic shift \(\tau\), \(R\) the transposition \(\rho\).

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r44 results}
\label{subsec:v490-r44-results}

\paragraph{Theoremlet v4.9.0-r44.1.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(a)] The linear and the antilinear connectors each form a \(24\)-dimensional complex space.
\item[(b)] The antilinear block from the kernel to the image of \(C\) is \(8\)-dimensional, with a lepton and a quark part of dimension \(4\) each,
and consists of weak doublets with \(Y/2=+\tfrac12\) (\(Q_{\rm em}=1,0\)) and \(Y/2=-\tfrac12\) (\(Q_{\rm em}=0,-1\)).
\item[(c)] The linear block from the kernel to the image is \(4\)-dimensional, lepton part only, with \(Y/2\in\{-\tfrac12,-\tfrac32\}\).
\item[(d)] No antilinear connector from the kernel to the image is gauge invariant; in the block from the kernel to the kernel exactly one is,
and it acts only on \(\nu^c\).
\item[(e)] (i) A Hermitian \(M\) commuting with \(P\) and \(R\) has at most two distinct eigenvalues.  (ii) A Hermitian \(M\) commuting with \(P\)
has the eigenvalues \(a+2|b|\cos(\theta+2\pi k/3)\), \(k=0,1,2\), with \(b=|b|e^{i\theta}\), generically distinct; \(K=\tfrac23\) holds for
\(|b|=a/\sqrt2\).  (iii) If the couplings for up and down both commute with \(P\), the mixing matrix \(|U_u^\dagger U_d|\) is a permutation
matrix.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Proof sketch.}
(a)--(d) by solving the commutation conditions on \(32\times32\) matrices and decomposing the blocks under \(SU(2)'\), \(Y\) and \(Q_{\rm em}\)
(script \texttt{llm/k85\_test\_h29.py}).  Linear maps cannot intertwine the colour triplet with its conjugate, so the linear quark block
vanishes.  (e) is elementary circulant algebra and was checked on \(200\) random matrices.  \(\square\)

\paragraph{Verdict and reading.}
H29a--e are confirmed.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item In the operator language of the Millennium matrix, mass terms are antilinear: they contain the frame change \(T\).  Linear off-diagonal
blocks of a colour-compatible Millennium matrix give no masses; they vanish for quarks and carry wrong charges for leptons.  In the model,
mass is a coupling between matter frame and tachyon frame, as the matter--tachyon spring of the spring theorem of the original paper.  The
couplings that do this carry exactly the quantum numbers of the Higgs doublet and its conjugate, for leptons and quarks.
\item None of these mass terms is gauge invariant; only a doublet expectation value turns them into masses.  The structure says which
breaking is needed, a doublet with \(Y/2=\tfrac12\), not why it occurs.  The only gauge-invariant connector is a Majorana term for \(\nu^c\),
which allows a see-saw reading for small neutrino masses (not computed).
\item With the full triole symmetry (\(\tau\) and \(\rho\)) on the slots there are at most two distinct masses; this is the reason for the
refutations of the earlier pre-registered mass tests.  Breaking only the reflection \(\rho\), the frame triole of v4.9.0-r38, and keeping
triality gives three distinct masses in exactly the Brannen form; the Koide value corresponds to \(|b|=a/\sqrt2\).  The Koide form of
v4.8.0-r10 is thus the mass form of a \(\mathbb Z_3\)-symmetric, \(\rho\)-broken coupling.  The ratio \(|b|/a\) and the phase \(\theta\) are not
explained.
\item Couplings that are both triality-symmetric are diagonalized by the same Fourier matrix and give no mixing.  Mixing requires breaking
triality itself, differently for up and down.
\item In the model each observed property has its breaking stage: mass the frame coupling (Higgs), mass splitting the breaking of the frame
reflection \(\rho\), mixing the breaking of triality \(\tau\).  Where these breakings come from is not said.
\end{enumerate}

\section*{v4.9.0-r45 Source-Only: Chirality Breaks the Reflection of the Triality Form}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{v4.9.0-r45 Source-Only: Chirality Breaks the Reflection of the Triality Form}
\label{sec:v490-r45-chirality-breaks-reflection}
% ============================================================================

\paragraph{Purpose.}
Layer v4.9.0-r44 found that mass splitting on the triality slots requires the reflection \(\rho\) to be broken and mixing requires triality
\(\tau\) to be broken.  The author asks whether these breakings follow from the structure.  This layer records the pre-registered test H30
(note \texttt{llm/Vorab\_Notiz\_H30\_Brechung\_aus\_Chiralitaet.md}, commit \texttt{d755a08}, fixed before computing; the identity
\(cL_{e_7}c=-R_{e_7}\) was seen analytically and is disclosed there).  Earlier layers are not rewritten.

\noindent\textbf{Source-only status.}
This v4.9.0-r45 layer is source-only.  It does not build a PDF, does not assign a new DOI,
and does not authorize a public release.  The v4.9.0 Version DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23534954}{10.5281/zenodo.23534954} and the Concept DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19210728}{10.5281/zenodo.19210728} are unchanged by it.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r45 two models}
\label{subsec:v490-r45-models}

Model~I: on \(\mathbb C\otimes\HH\otimes\OO^3\) the chirality projector \(C_{\rm ch}=\tfrac12(1+iL_{e_7})\) of v4.9.0-r42 acts in the same way on
every slot.  Model~II: the chirality operator is \(i(A,B,C)\) with \((A,B,C)\) in the triality algebra \(\mathfrak g\) of v4.9.0-r37 and
\(A=L_{e_7}\); \(B\) and \(C\) are then fixed.  Here \(c\) is octonionic conjugation, and \(\rho(x,y,z)=(\bar x,\bar z,\bar y)\).

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r45 results}
\label{subsec:v490-r45-results}

\paragraph{Theoremlet v4.9.0-r45.1.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(a)] \(cL_{e_7}c=-R_{e_7}\) and \(cR_{e_7}c=-L_{e_7}\).  The plain exchange \((x,y,z)\mapsto(x,z,y)\) is not a symmetry of \(t\); the
transposition symmetries of \(t\) contain \(c\).
\item[(b)] Of the six symmetries \(\tau^k\rho^\varepsilon\) of \(t\), exactly \(1,\tau,\tau^2\) preserve the chirality projector of Model~I; \(\rho\),
\(\tau\rho\), \(\tau^2\rho\) map it to \(\tfrac12(1-iR_{e_7})\), the anomalous choice of v4.9.0-r43.
\item[(c)] \((L_{e_7},L_{e_7},L_{e_7})\notin\mathfrak g\).  The element of \(\mathfrak g\) with first component \(L_{e_7}\) is
\((L_{e_7},R_{e_7},-L_{e_7}-R_{e_7})\); it is not \(\tau\)-invariant.
\item[(d)] In Model~II the chirality operator is \(iR_{e_7}\) on slot~2 and \(-i(L_{e_7}+R_{e_7})\) on slot~3, which is not an involution.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Proof sketch.}
(a) from \(\overline{e_7\bar x}=x\bar e_7=-xe_7\); the rest by explicit matrices and the linear system for \(\mathfrak g\) (script
\texttt{llm/k86\_test\_h30.py}, deviations at most \(10^{-15}\)).  (c): a diagonal element of \(\mathfrak g\) lies in \(\mathfrak g_2\)
(v4.9.0-r37.1(c)), and \(L_{e_7}\) is not a derivation.  \(\square\)

\paragraph{Verdict and reading.}
H30a--d are confirmed.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item The breaking of \(\rho\) follows from the structure.  The transposition symmetries of the triality form contain octonionic
conjugation, conjugation exchanges left and right multiplication, and anomaly cancellation chose the left (v4.9.0-r43).  So \(\rho\) cannot be
a symmetry of the chiral theory; combining it with the frame change \(T\) does not help, since \(T\) changes the sign, not the side.  The
condition of v4.9.0-r44 for mass splitting is therefore met structurally: the slot couplings are generically \(\rho\)-asymmetric, with three
distinct masses in Brannen form.  This also explains why the natural potentials of the earlier mass tests, which did not know chirality, gave
at most two masses.  How strongly \(\rho\) is broken, the phase \(\theta\) and the ratio \(|b|/a\), is not fixed.
\item Triality \(\tau\) survives, but only in Model~I, where the slots are equivalent copies with equal chirality.  The triality-covariant
chirality of Model~II fails: slot~2 would be anomalous and slot~3 would carry no chirality.  The generations therefore cannot be the three
inequivalent triality representations with their own chirality, as read in v4.9.0-r37; they must be equivalent copies, with \(\tau\) permuting
them.  This agrees with the equal quantum numbers of all generations.
\item Mixing stays open: in Model~I \(\tau\) is preserved and, by v4.9.0-r44, there is no mixing.  The breaking needed for mixing has to come
from a different, weaker source, which the structure has not supplied so far.
\end{enumerate}

\section*{v4.9.0-r46 Source-Only: Mixed Readouts and the Breaking of Triality}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{v4.9.0-r46 Source-Only: Mixed Readouts and the Breaking of Triality}
\label{sec:v490-r46-readouts-triality-breaking}
% ============================================================================

\paragraph{Purpose.}
Layer v4.9.0-r45 derived the breaking of the reflection \(\rho\) from chirality, while triality \(\tau\) survives, so that no mixing arises
(v4.9.0-r44).  This layer records the pre-registered test H31 (note \texttt{llm/Vorab\_Notiz\_H31\_Tau\_Brechung\_Readouts.md}, commit
\texttt{ea74447}, fixed before computing) of whether the mixed readouts of
Definitions~\ref{def:binary-mask-registry-mixed-readouts} and~\ref{def:guarded-two-sided-mixed-qam-readout} can break \(\tau\).  The note
discloses that (a) follows from commutativity and that (c) is general perturbation theory.  Earlier layers are not rewritten.

\noindent\textbf{Source-only status.}
This v4.9.0-r46 layer is source-only.  It does not build a PDF, does not assign a new DOI,
and does not authorize a public release.  The v4.9.0 Version DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23534954}{10.5281/zenodo.23534954} and the Concept DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19210728}{10.5281/zenodo.19210728} are unchanged by it.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r46 setting}
\label{subsec:v490-r46-setting}

On the slot space \(\mathbb C^3\) let \(P=\tau\), and let \(M_u\), \(M_d\) be Hermitian \(\mathbb Z_3\)-circulants (\(\rho\)-broken, \(\tau\)-symmetric, as
after v4.9.0-r45); the mixing is \(|V|=|U_u^\dagger U_d|\).  A \emph{shift readout} is built from the level readouts \(P^{2^j}\) of v4.9.0-r39:
products (reading \(\Sigma\)), averages over generated subgroups (reading \(\vee\)), and selector contractions \(\sum\chi_{mn}P^{2^m}P^{2^n}\).  A
\emph{kind mask}, with the readout kind taken to be the slot, is a diagonal projector \(D_S\), \(\emptyset\neq S\subsetneq\{1,2,3\}\), entering as
\(M+\delta D_S\).

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r46 results}
\label{subsec:v490-r46-results}

\paragraph{Theoremlet v4.9.0-r46.1.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(a)] Every shift readout commutes with \(\tau\); added to \(M_u\) and \(M_d\) it leaves \(|V|\) a permutation matrix.
\item[(b)] Kind masks do not commute with \(\tau\); for \(\delta>0\) and generic \(M_u,M_d\), \(|V|\) is not a permutation matrix, even with the same
mask and weight for up and down.
\item[(c)] For small \(\delta\) the deviation of \(|V|\) from a permutation is linear in \(\delta\) and bounded by
\(\tfrac{\delta}{3}\bigl(\mathrm{gap}_u^{-1}+\mathrm{gap}_d^{-1}\bigr)\), with the smallest spacings of the eigenvalues.
\item[(d)] Kind masks are real and commute with the frame change \(T\).
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Proof sketch.}
(a): shift readouts lie in the group ring \(\mathbb C[\mathbb Z_3]\), which is commutative, so they are circulant; circulants are diagonalized by
one Fourier matrix.  (b)--(d) checked on \(200\) random cases each (script \texttt{llm/k87\_test\_h31.py}); the factor \(\tfrac13\) in (c) is the
size of the entries of a one-slot mask in the Fourier basis.  \(\square\)

\paragraph{Verdict and reading.}
H31a--d are confirmed; (a) and (c) are elementary.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item The mixed readouts as they are formed from the Hilbert shift produce no mixing: every readout built only from the generation shifts
commutes with \(\tau\).  This is a genuine prohibition.
\item Only a readout that distinguishes generations as readout kinds breaks \(\tau\).  Then mixing arises, already with the same mask for up and
down, and it is small when the mask is weak and the masses are far apart.  This agrees qualitatively with small quark mixing at a strong mass
hierarchy and with larger mixing where masses lie close, as for neutrinos.  No numbers follow.
\item Mixing is measurable and not an artefact of an observer.  A mixing from readout can therefore only mean that the generation-selective
mask is part of the couplings, of the structure in which one reads out, not a dependence on who measures.  Where such a mask comes from in the
structure is open.
\end{enumerate}

\label{subsec:v490-r46-frames}

\section*{v4.9.0-r47 Source-Only: A Generation Mask from the Triality-Covariant Chirality}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{v4.9.0-r47 Source-Only: A Generation Mask from the Triality-Covariant Chirality}
\label{sec:v490-r47-mask-from-triality}
% ============================================================================

\paragraph{Purpose.}
Layer v4.9.0-r46 found that mixing needs a mask that distinguishes generations.  Layer v4.9.0-r45 showed that a triality-covariant chirality
(Model~II) is impossible as an exact symmetry.  This layer records the pre-registered test H32 (note
\texttt{llm/Vorab\_Notiz\_H32\_Maske\_aus\_Triality.md}, commit \texttt{65c5b44}, fixed before computing) of whether the deviation of
Model~II from Model~I supplies such a mask.  The slot weights were derived beforehand from the sector table of v4.9.0-r43 and are disclosed
in the note.  Earlier layers are not rewritten.

\noindent\textbf{Source-only status.}
This v4.9.0-r47 layer is source-only.  It does not build a PDF, does not assign a new DOI,
and does not authorize a public release.  The v4.9.0 Version DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23534954}{10.5281/zenodo.23534954} and the Concept DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19210728}{10.5281/zenodo.19210728} are unchanged by it.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r47 model assumption}
\label{subsec:v490-r47-assumption}

The exact chirality is that of Model~I.  The deviation \(\Delta=J_{\rm II}-J_{\rm I}\), on slot \(i\) given by \(\Delta_i=i(B_i-L_{e_7})\) with
\(B=(L_{e_7},R_{e_7},-L_{e_7}-R_{e_7})\), acts as a weak perturbation of the couplings with small weight \(\varepsilon\): a slot coupling \(M\) becomes
\(M+\varepsilon(WM+MW)\), with \(W\) the diagonal of slot weights of the sector, and the right-handed partner is read in the T frame
(v4.9.0-r41.1(c)).  This assumption is not derived.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r47 results}
\label{subsec:v490-r47-results}

\paragraph{Theoremlet v4.9.0-r47.1.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(a)] \(\Delta_i\) is scalar on each \(Q\)-sector, with slot weights \((0,0,-3)\) on sector \(0\), \((0,2,1)\) on \(\tfrac13\), \((0,-2,-1)\) on
\(\tfrac23\), \((0,0,3)\) on \(1\).
\item[(b)] \(T\Delta T^{-1}=-\Delta\); the weights are antisymmetric under \(q\leftrightarrow1-q\), so the partner read in the T frame carries the same
weights as the left-handed particle: \(W_q=\mathrm{diag}(0,-2,-1)\) for up and down quarks, \(W_l=\mathrm{diag}(0,0,-3)\) for leptons.
\item[(c)] \(W_q\) and \(W_l\) do not commute with \(\tau\); with \(\mathbb Z_3\)-circulant Hermitian \(M_u\), \(M_d\) and the same mask \(W_q\) the mixing
is non-zero for \(\varepsilon>0\) in all \(200\) random cases.  It is linear in \(\varepsilon\) in \(196\) of them at the pre-registered values
\(\varepsilon=10^{-3},10^{-4}\).
\item[(d)] In the Fourier basis of the generations all off-diagonal entries have equal size, \(1/\sqrt3\) for \(W_q\) and \(1\) for \(W_l\); the
ratio is \(\sqrt3\).
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Proof sketch.}
(a), (b) from \(iL_{e_7},iR_{e_7}=\pm1\) on the sectors (v4.9.0-r43) and checked by matrices; (c), (d) numerically (script
\texttt{llm/k88\_test\_h32.py}).  A check after the evaluation found the four non-linear cases nearly degenerate (relative gap at most
\(3\cdot10^{-3}\), median \(7\cdot10^{-2}\)); at \(\varepsilon=10^{-6},10^{-7}\) all \(200\) are linear.  \(\square\)

\paragraph{Verdict and reading.}
H32a, b and d are confirmed.  H32c is refuted as stated, since linearity fails in four of \(200\) cases at the pre-registered \(\varepsilon\); the
mixing is non-zero in all cases, and linearity holds in the perturbative range \(\varepsilon\ll\) gap.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item Under the model assumption the generation mask comes from the structure.  The triality-covariant chirality is forbidden as an exact
symmetry, but its deviation from equal chirality is a fixed number per slot on every sector.  The shape of the mask is fixed,
\(\mathrm{diag}(0,-2,-1)\) for quarks and \(\mathrm{diag}(0,0,-3)\) for leptons; only \(\varepsilon\) is chosen.  Up and down share the mask, and
mixing still arises because their couplings differ (v4.9.0-r46).
\item In the generation basis the mask is democratic, with all pairs coupled equally, and \(\sqrt3\) stronger for leptons than for quarks.  At
equal \(\varepsilon\) this means stronger lepton than quark mixing, enhanced further by the close neutrino masses.  Qualitatively this is the
direction of observation; no numbers follow.
\item On slot~1 the weight is always zero, since both chiralities agree there.  Which slot is the reference is a choice of the kind met in
v4.9.0-r38.  In this picture the breaking of triality comes from the choice of a reference slot.
\item The model assumption, \(\varepsilon\), the circulant parameters and hence all numbers remain open.
\end{enumerate}

\section*{v4.9.0-r48 Source-Only: The Charge Sectors Derived from the Octonion Product in Lean}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{v4.9.0-r48 Source-Only: The Charge Sectors Derived from the Octonion Product in Lean}
\label{sec:v490-r48-sectors-formal}
% ============================================================================

\paragraph{Purpose.}
The Lean module \texttt{formal/QSFormal/Ladungen.lean} (layers v4.9.0-r34 to r47) sets the four charge sectors as data: the sign pairs
\((\ell,r)\) of \(iL_{e_7}\), \(iR_{e_7}\) and the colour dimensions \(1,3,3,1\) are definitions there, taken from the numerics of
\texttt{llm/k75} and \texttt{llm/k84}.  This layer records the new module \texttt{formal/QSFormal/Sektoren.lean}, which derives these tables
from the octonion product of \texttt{formal/QSFormal/Dixon.lean} (Cayley--Dickson, as in \texttt{llm/k22}).  It makes no new prediction,
so no pre-registration note is needed; it secures results of v4.9.0-r34, r35, r42, r43 and r47.  Earlier layers are not rewritten.

\noindent\textbf{Source-only status.}
This v4.9.0-r48 layer is source-only.  It does not build a PDF, does not assign a new DOI,
and does not authorize a public release.  The v4.9.0 Version DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23534954}{10.5281/zenodo.23534954} and the Concept DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19210728}{10.5281/zenodo.19210728} are unchanged by it.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r48 setting}
\label{subsec:v490-r48-setting}

On \(\mathbb C\otimes\mathbb O\) with the outer unit \(i\) (the \(\mathbb C\) factor of the Dixon store, layer v4.9.0-r35) and the
conjugation \(T\) of that factor, \(L\) and \(R\) are left and right multiplication by \(e_7\), acting on both parts.  For a sector
\(s=(\ell,r)\in\{(1,1),(-1,1),(1,-1),(-1,-1)\}\) put
\[
P_s=\tfrac14\,(1+\ell\, iL)(1+r\, iR),\qquad 6Q=3-iL-2\,iR,
\]
\[
\Delta_1=0,\qquad \Delta_2=iR-iL,\qquad \Delta_3=-2\,iL-iR,
\]
the last three being the deviations \(\Delta_i=i(B_i-L_{e_7})\) of layer v4.9.0-r47.  The complex unit of every statement below is the outer
unit \(i\); the inner unit \(J=L_{e_7}\) is not used (rule~7 of the round format).

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r48 results}
\label{subsec:v490-r48-results}

\paragraph{Theoremlet v4.9.0-r48.1.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(a)] \((iL)^2=(iR)^2=1\) and \(iL\,iR=iR\,iL\) for all \(x\in\mathbb C\otimes\mathbb O\).
\item[(b)] Each \(P_s\) is a projector, the four are pairwise orthogonal and sum to \(1\), and on the image of \(P_s\) one has \(iL=\ell\),
\(iR=r\).
\item[(c)] Over the basis \(e_0,\dots,e_7\): \(\operatorname{tr}L=\operatorname{tr}R=0\), \(\operatorname{tr}(LR)=4\), and
\(\operatorname{tr}P_s=1,3,3,1\) for \(s=(1,1),(-1,1),(1,-1),(-1,-1)\); this is the colour dimension of the sector.
\item[(d)] On the image of \(P_s\), \(6Q\) acts as \(3-\ell-2r\), i.e.\ \(Q=0,\tfrac13,\tfrac23,1\), and \(\Delta_2\), \(\Delta_3\) act as
\(r-\ell\), \(-2\ell-r\): the slot weights \((0,0,-3)\), \((0,2,1)\), \((0,-2,-1)\), \((0,0,3)\) of v4.9.0-r47.1(a).
\item[(e)] \(T\,iL=-iL\,T\), \(T\,iR=-iR\,T\), and \(T P_{(\ell,r)}=P_{(-\ell,-r)}T\): the frame \(T\) is charge conjugation \(Q\mapsto1-Q\).
\item[(f)] The colour rotation \(\sigma\) of v4.9.0-r36 commutes on the basis with \(iL\), \(iR\) and every \(P_s\).
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Proof sketch.}
All items are checked in Lean~4.35.0 with the Lean core only (no Mathlib): (a), (b), (d), (e) for all \(x\) by expanding the product and
\texttt{omega}, (c) and (f) by \texttt{decide}.  Only the standard axioms are used.  Two steps are standard and not formalised: that the rank
of a projector is its trace, and the extension of (f) from the basis to all \(x\) by linearity (as for \texttt{sigma\_respects} in
v4.9.0-r36).  \(\square\)

\paragraph{Reading.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item The tables \texttt{sectors}, \texttt{colourDim}, \texttt{q6} and \texttt{weights} of the module \texttt{Ladungen} are no longer
settings but properties of the octonion product.  The integer arithmetic built on them in that module (hypercharge, anomaly traces, the
content of a generation, the Eisenstein norms of the mask) therefore rests on the structure, under the reading of v4.9.0-r35 that the
\(\mathbb C\) factor is the outer unit.
\item The sign conventions of the numerics agree with the formal ones: the sector \((1,1)\) has charge \(0\) and rank \(1\).
\item Not formalised remain the physical readings (which sector is which particle, the choice of chirality and offset in v4.9.0-r43, the
model assumption of v4.9.0-r47) and all numbers for masses and mixing.
\end{enumerate}

\section*{v4.9.0-r49 Source-Only: The Axis \texorpdfstring{\(e_7\)}{e7} Carries B--L, Not the Electric Charge}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{v4.9.0-r49 Source-Only: The Axis e7 Carries B--L, Not the Electric Charge}
\label{sec:v490-r49-e7-axis-b-minus-l}
% ============================================================================

\paragraph{Purpose.}
After layer v4.9.0-r48 a physical reading was proposed: the charge is the coupling to the axis \(e_7\), colour is the rotations about it, and
the axis is the photon axis of the Companion.  This layer records the pre-registered test H33 (note
\texttt{llm/Vorab\_Notiz\_H33\_Photon\_Achse\_e7.md}, commit \texttt{ec06de3}, fixed before computing) of that reading.  The note discloses that the
outcome was seen analytically beforehand, so the computation is a check, not a prediction.  Earlier layers are not rewritten.

\noindent\textbf{Source-only status.}
This v4.9.0-r49 layer is source-only.  It does not build a PDF, does not assign a new DOI,
and does not authorize a public release.  The v4.9.0 Version DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23534954}{10.5281/zenodo.23534954} and the Concept DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19210728}{10.5281/zenodo.19210728} are unchanged by it.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r49 text finding}
\label{subsec:v490-r49-text}

The main body uses the plane \(\operatorname{span}(1,e_7)\) three times: as the photon kernel \(K_3\), the image of the compensator \(C\)
(``the singlet \(\mathbb C\subset\mathbb O\), which is the photon kernel \(K_3\) sector''); as the carrier of \(\nu_R\) in the table of
Theorem~\ref{thm:three-generations}; and as the plane of the symmetric generation point \(\omega\), ``the complex plane at the end of the
cascade'' (v4.8.0-r15.2), whose final stratum \(S^2\) is the photon ground state.  Complex unit below: the outer unit \(i\) (rule~7 of the
round format), as in v4.9.0-r48.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r49 results}
\label{subsec:v490-r49-results}

\paragraph{Theoremlet v4.9.0-r49.1.}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(a)] \(\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb C}(1,e_7)\) is exactly the sum of the sectors \((\ell,r)=(1,1)\) and \((-1,-1)\), i.e.\ \(Q=0\) and
\(Q=1\); on it \(L_{e_7}=R_{e_7}\).
\item[(b)] On the sixteen states of one generation (content of v4.9.0-r42.1(b)) one has \(2(Q-\tfrac12)=B-L\), and with the chirality and
offset of v4.9.0-r43
\[
\tfrac{Y}{2}=\tfrac{B-L}{2}+T_3^R,\qquad Q_{\rm em}=T_3^L+T_3^R+\tfrac{B-L}{2},\qquad T_3^L=C\,T_3,\quad T_3^R=(1-C)\,T_3 .
\]
\item[(c)] No element \(a+b\,iL_{e_7}+c\,iR_{e_7}+d\,iL_{e_7}iR_{e_7}\) acts as \(Q_{\rm em}\) on a generation; the best one misses by
\(\tfrac12\), the \(T_3\) splitting inside a doublet.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Proof sketch.}
(a) numerically from the projectors of v4.9.0-r48 (script \texttt{llm/k89\_test\_h33.py}).  (b) is integer arithmetic on the table; (c)
holds because every such element is constant on a sector (v4.9.0-r48.1(b)) while \(Q_{\rm em}\) is not.  (b) and (c) are also checked in
Lean (\texttt{b\_minus\_l}, \texttt{qem\_not\_sector\_function} in \texttt{formal/QSFormal/Sektoren.lean}).  \(\square\)

\paragraph{Verdict and reading.}
H33a, b and c are confirmed, so by the pre-registered criterion the proposed reading is refuted as stated.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item The axis \(e_7\) carries \(B-L\) (and, through \(iL_{e_7}\), the chirality), not the electric charge.  The photon needs the weak
isospin \(T_3\) from the \(\mathbb H\) factor as well; it is the mixture of the \(e_7\) direction with the \(T_3\) direction.  Colour, the
rotations fixing \(e_7\), commutes with \(B-L\).
\item The charge formula is the left--right symmetric one, \(Q_{\rm em}=T_3^L+T_3^R+(B-L)/2\), with \(T_3^R\) carried by the right-handed
partners read in the T frame.  Hypercharge is the combination \(Y/2=T_3^R+(B-L)/2\).
\item The plane \(\operatorname{span}(1,e_7)\) is used in the main body both as the photon kernel and as the carrier of \(\nu_R\); in the
outer frame it carries the lepton singlets \(Q=0\) and \(Q=1\).  A state plane and the photon as a generator are different objects.  This
is a double use of the kind found in v4.9.0-r36 and a candidate for a status note.
\item Not tested: whether \(e_7\) serves as the reference axis of the frames (photon frame as neutral axis, v4.9.0-r01).  On the singlet
plane the \(U(1)\) fibre of \(S^3\to S^2\) (Theorem~\ref{thm:hopf}) is the phase \(e^{\theta e_7}\), i.e.\ in distribution D the
\(U(1)_{B-L}\).
\end{enumerate}

\section*{v4.9.0-r50 Source-Only: The Mask of r47 Does Not Produce the Observed Mixing}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{v4.9.0-r50 Source-Only: The Mask of r47 Does Not Produce the Observed Mixing}
\label{sec:v490-r50-mixing-test}
% ============================================================================

\paragraph{Purpose.}
Layer v4.9.0-r47 derived the shape of a generation mask under a model assumption and read it qualitatively as stronger lepton than quark
mixing.  This layer records the pre-registered quantitative test H34 (note \texttt{llm/Vorab\_Notiz\_H34\_Mischung\_aus\_Maske.md}, commit
\texttt{8d8b761}, fixed before computing) against the measured CKM and PMNS mixing.  Earlier layers are not rewritten.

\noindent\textbf{Source-only status.}
This v4.9.0-r50 layer is source-only.  It does not build a PDF, does not assign a new DOI,
and does not authorize a public release.  The v4.9.0 Version DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23534954}{10.5281/zenodo.23534954} and the Concept DOI
\href{https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19210728}{10.5281/zenodo.19210728} are unchanged by it.

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r50 model and data}
\label{subsec:v490-r50-model}

For \(X\in\{u,d,e,\nu\}\): \(M_X=C_X+\varepsilon(W_XC_X+C_XW_X)\) with \(C_X\) Hermitian and \(\mathbb Z_3\)-circulant, \(W_u=W_d=W_q\),
\(W_e=W_\nu=W_l\) from v4.9.0-r47, one common \(\varepsilon\ge0\), Dirac neutrinos.  Masses are the moduli of the eigenvalues; mixing is
\(U_u^\dagger U_d\) and \(U_e^\dagger U_\nu\).  Data fixed in the note: running masses at \(M_Z\) (Huang and Zhou 2021), neutrino
mass splittings and \(3\sigma\) ranges of NuFIT~6.0 (normal ordering), CKM magnitudes of the PDG 2024.  All solution branches are followed
from \(\varepsilon=0\) (24 assignments per sector) in steps of \(0.005\) up to \(\varepsilon=3\).

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r50 results}
\label{subsec:v490-r50-results}

\paragraph{Theoremlet v4.9.0-r50.1 (numerical).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item[(a)] Solutions with the measured quark masses exist only up to \(\varepsilon\approx0.06\) (up) and \(0.125\) (down).  In none of the
\(7488\) combinations of \(\varepsilon\) and branch pair are \(|V_{us}|\), \(|V_{cb}|\), \(|V_{ub}|\) all within \(\pm20\%\); the closest case is
\(96\%\) off.
\item[(b)] Hence no common \(\varepsilon\) exists for quarks and leptons in the sense of H34b.  The leptons alone, with their own
\(\varepsilon\), reach the \(3\sigma\) ranges in no case either; the closest case is up to \(71\%\) outside.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Proof sketch.}
Script \texttt{llm/k90\_test\_h34.py} with output \texttt{llm/k90\_out.txt}.  The first run stopped after H34a in a diagnostic print; only that
print and the treatment of \(m_1=0\) were corrected, and both outputs are kept.  The bound on \(\varepsilon\) was confirmed by random multistarts.
The lepton statement in (b) is a check after the evaluation.  \(\square\)

\paragraph{Verdict and reading.}
H34a and H34b are refuted; H34c does not count.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item The model assumption of v4.9.0-r47, the mask as a weak perturbation of circulant couplings, does not produce the measured mixing,
neither for quarks nor for leptons.  The qualitative reading of r47 (stronger lepton mixing) is withdrawn as a quantitative statement.
\item The reason is structural.  For a nearly rank-one coupling the term \(\varepsilon^2WCW\) creates a mass of order
\(\tfrac23\varepsilon^2m_t\) (quarks) or \(2\varepsilon^2m_\tau\) (leptons), so the mass hierarchy forces small \(\varepsilon\); at small
\(\varepsilon\) the democratic mask cannot produce \(|V_{us}|\gg|V_{cb}|\gg|V_{ub}|\) or the large PMNS angles.
\item Unaffected are v4.9.0-r47.1(a), (b), (d): the shape of the mask, its frame behaviour and its Fourier form.  Mixing needs another
form of coupling or a further breaking.
\end{enumerate}

\label{subsec:v490-r50-frames}

\end{center}

\subsection*{v4.9.0-r50 register entries}
\label{subsec:v490-r50-register}

\toprule
